# Survey: test-time compute and reasoning in LLMs

Factual cache for `inputs/reasoning-legacy.md`. Descriptive only — salience and quality judgements live in
`outputs/reasoning-legacy.md`. Renamed from `reasoning` on 2026-10-09, before a fresh coreset pass on the same input;
the log entries below keep the names they were written with.

## Retrieval log

### Pass 2026-09-22

  - [Yue et al.](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.13837): 66 references screened in full, about 30 in scope. Forward citations

  - [Korbak et al.](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.11473): 28 arXiv-resolvable references screened in full (9 further
    reference entries are lab blog posts or internal report sections with no paper ID, so are not screenable via Asta).
    Forward citations screened in two rounds — roughly 50 under an initial `2026-06-01:` restriction, then all 100
    titles returned under `2026-01-01:`. That query returned exactly 100, almost certainly truncated at the limit, and
    was **not paginated beyond 100**.
  - [Snell et al.](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.03314): **not traversed**.

- **New papers added:** 150.

- **Dimensions:** the four cross-cutting columns declared in `inputs/reasoning.md` — `evidence`, `measures`,
  `elicitation`, `pressure` — are filled for all 150 rows. Tallies, which `tools/check.py` recomputes from the table:

  - `evidence`: empirical 122, position 8, proven 8, fitted 8, derived 4
  - `measures`: capability 103, monitorability 21, faithfulness 11, n/a 8, control-protocol 7
  - `elicitation`: n/a 98, elicited 48, spontaneous 2, observational 2
  - `pressure`: n/a 106, prompt-level 32, training-incidental 6, none 4, monitor-in-reward 2

  **These values were derived from each row's existing text, not independently from the papers.** For a
  `snippet`-sourced row that text came from body text, so the value is traceable but second-hand; for an
  `abstract`-sourced row it inherits the abstract's limits. Under the definition of "verify" in `CLAUDE.md`, which
  requires dimensions to be re-derived from body text, **no row currently counts as fully verified** — the other six
  columns are, for 123 rows, but the dimensions are not. Closing that is a corpus-wide verification pass.

  Judgement calls worth knowing about, since a downstream claim rests on the `elicitation` tally:
  [2412.04984](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.04984) is tagged `elicited` for its headline design but also documents one
  spontaneous instance, so 48 slightly overcounts the elicited side. [2505.05410](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.05410) has
  both a prompt-level and an RL arm and is tagged `prompt-level` for the headline metric.
  [2512.18311](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.18311), [2603.30036](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.30036) and
  [2601.23086](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.23086) are tagged `training-incidental` but each has a condition arguably
  closer to `monitor-in-reward`. Note `pressure` records what was tested, not the outcome: 2512.18311 is
  `training-incidental` while reporting *no* degradation.

- **Sourcing:** 123 rows are `snippet`-sourced, checked against method and results text. 27 remain `abstract`-sourced.
  Those 27 are not outstanding work: 25 are 2026 preprints Asta has not indexed for open-access full text, and 2 are
  survey papers with no quantitative claim to check. Each such row states this in place, so the `source` column is
  authoritative row by row.

- **Discovery method:** WebSearch was used to locate candidates alongside citation traversal; every row was then
  verified against Semantic Scholar metadata via Asta (`get_paper_batch`) and characterised from `snippet_search` body
  text where available. Three entries whose abstracts were unavailable through that path
  ([2509.04259](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.04259), [2510.09462](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.09462),
  [2602.14095](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.14095)) were verified by fetching the arXiv abstract page directly. No row
  rests on WebSearch alone.

- **Stopped because:** Neither stopping condition was met. This pass is **incomplete** and coverage should be treated as
  broad but open. One of three seeds remains untraversed, and one forward-citation query was truncated at 100 results
  without pagination. Saturation was measured rather than assumed, and it is low in both directions: about 37% for the
  Yue seed (24 of ~65 in-scope candidates already present) and roughly 29% for the Korbak seed (6 of ~21), against a
  threshold of 80%. The forward-citation sets are producing new in-scope material faster than the per-sub-pass budget
  can absorb.

**Known gaps:**

- Reference and forward-citation traversal of [Snell et al.](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.03314): not done.
- Korbak forward citations capped at 100 returned results with no pagination; the true 2026 citing set is larger.
- Query-side saturation was not tested: no keyword formulation was run twice far enough apart to measure whether it had
  stopped returning new material.
- WebSearch discovery surfaces recent and widely-discussed work rather than foundational work. Backward traversal
  recovered papers that arguably should never have been missing, including
  [GRPO/DeepSeekMath](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.03300), [DAPO](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.14476),
  [STaR](https://arxiv.org/abs/2203.14465), [Kimi k1.5](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.12599),
  [the original CoT prompting paper](https://arxiv.org/abs/2201.11903) and
  [the founding AI-control paper](https://arxiv.org/abs/2312.06942). Blocks whose seeds have not been traversed
  backwards should be assumed to carry the same bias.
- Non-English-language venues, and OpenReview-only submissions without arXiv postings, were not searched.
- The `venue+year` column records the venue Semantic Scholar reports. Where S2 lists a venue but an ambiguous year, the
  arXiv posting month is given in parentheses. Two rows note venue claims taken from a lab website rather than a
  publisher.
- On the elicited-versus-spontaneous imbalance in the safety blocks: this is **not yet an admissible absence claim**. It
  was arrived at by classifying what retrieval happened to return, which tells you about the retrieval and not about the
  field. No dedicated adversarial search has been run against it. Framings such as "in the wild", "deployed",
  "observational" and "audit" have not been searched, and the one strong observational case in the corpus
  ([2605.31170](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.31170)) was found through citation traversal rather than keyword search,
  which is itself evidence that keyword search on this topic misses that framing.

### Pass 2026-09-23

A seed-traversal pass, run to close the gap recorded in the frontier after pass 2026-09-22. The within-set in-degree
criterion, applied retroactively to the 150 rows then present, nominated three papers that had never been traversed:
[DeepSeekMath / GRPO](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.03300) (rank 2), [Snell et al.](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.03314) (rank
8\) and [DeepSeek-R1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.12948) (rank 9-equal). Each was traversed by a separate agent; the three
sub-entries below record each traversal independently.

The purpose is a coverage guarantee rather than volume: the question being answered is whether the existing corpus is a
subset of what these seeds' neighbourhoods contain.

**Outcome.** It is not. Each sub-pass hit its 15-row cap, adding 45 rows in total, all `snippet`-sourced. Measured
saturation per seed:

| seed | already present / in-scope screened | saturation |
| -- | -- | -- |
| 2402.03300 (GRPO) | 21/190 | 11.1% |
| 2408.03314 (Snell) | 9/199 | 4.5% |
| 2501.12948 (R1) | 51/440 | 11.6% |

The three figures are not summed. Each was measured against the 150-row snapshot taken at the start, and the three
candidate pools overlap by an amount nobody measured. The stopping rule is not met on either condition. Traversal is
incomplete because every forward-citation set was sampled rather than exhausted. Saturation is an order of magnitude
below the 80% threshold.

Two limits apply to all three figures. First, in-scope judgements were made from titles alone, so near-miss exclusions
went unverified; this is the selection false-negative risk described in `CLAUDE.md`. Second, Semantic Scholar's citation
graph is itself incomplete for this literature. DeepSeek-R1 is split across two records, and many survey rows that build
directly on R1 do not appear among its indexed citers. A traversal of this index therefore cannot deliver a full
coverage guarantee, only a lower bound on how much is missing.

**Where the corpus is thin.** Backward references were missing the foundational verifier and search literature: Cobbe et
al.'s GSM8K verifiers, Uesato et al.'s process-versus-outcome comparison, Math-Shepherd and Tree of Thoughts. They are
now added. Forward citations were the weaker side for all three seeds. Saturation was lowest for the capability blocks
(R1: 7.8% on hand-offs to A–E and K, against 14.9% on the theory and safety blocks). The R1 agent notes that the
hand-off denominator came from keyword filters rather than title-by-title judgement, so the two figures are not strictly
comparable.

- **Errored queries:** none. One call hit a transient `ConnectionRefusedError`: the first 2510.13786 query, "sigmoidal

    only if they were among the 200 most-cited.
- **In-scope criterion used:** a candidate was in-scope if its title indicates work on the mechanics of RL post-training
  for reasoning, test-time compute, verification or reward design, self-training, forgetting, or reasoning-safety, i.e.
  the themes of blocks A–P. Excluded: domain applications (vision, video, driving, GUI, medical, recommendation), model
  technical reports, training frameworks and infrastructure, datasets and benchmarks, and agentic-search applications.
  This is a judgement call made from titles.

    7 foundational RL and alignment papers (PPO, GAE, DPO, InstructGPT, RRHF, PRO, Weak-to-Strong) whose scope is a
    judgement call. Counting only the 10 reasoning-specific ones gives 2/10 = 20%.
  - Forward citations: of 173 in-scope candidates, 19 were already present, 19/173 = 11.0%. Within the 2026 slice alone
    the figure is 5/67.
  - Combined: 21/190 = 11.1%.
  - Three in-scope references (2110.14168, 2305.10601, 2312.08935) were added concurrently by another agent during this
    pass. They are counted as not present, because they were absent from the snapshot.
- **Corrections to existing rows:** none.
- **Stopped because:** the 15-row cap was reached.
  - Traversal of this seed is complete at title level for the references. It is partial for forward citations: 754 of
    8,950 screened, 4,677 fetched.
  - Saturation is 11.1%, far below the 80% threshold. The coverage question this sub-pass was asked therefore has a
    clear answer. **The existing survey is not a subset of this seed's neighbourhood.** The GRPO forward-citation set
    contains a large body of directly relevant work that was absent, including prior art central to blocks C, D and E:
    spurious-reward and contamination probes, CoT-Pass@K, entropy-mechanism work, and GSPO / ScaleRL.
  - The 151 in-scope candidates not added are listed in the frontier, as C/D/E deferrals or as hand-offs to other
    blocks.

#### Seed: 2408.03314 (Snell et al., compute-optimal test-time scaling)

- **Preflight:** `snippet_search` ok | `search_papers_by_relevance` ok | `get_paper` ok

- **Seeds:** given by the main session — [Snell et al.](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.03314), nominated in pass 2026-09-22
  and not traversed until now.

- **Queries run:**

  - `get_paper` on ARXIV:2408.03314 with `references` fields. Semantic Scholar resolved only 32 reference entries, so
    the complete 51-entry bibliography was read from the arXiv HTML version (`arxiv.org/html/2408.03314`) and screened
    in full. That page was used for discovery only; nothing was characterised from it.
  - `get_citations` on ARXIV:2408.03314, limit 100, in four date windows: `2026-06-01:`, `2024-08-01:2024-12-31`,
    `2025-01-01:2025-06-30`, `2026-01-01:2026-05-31`.
  - Preflight calls: `snippet_search` "compute-optimal test-time scaling strategy" (scoped to 2408.03314);
    `search_papers_by_relevance` "best-of-N verifier test-time compute" (limit 3); `get_paper` ARXIV:2408.03314.
  - `get_paper_batch` metadata lookup for the 15 papers added.
  - Per-paper `snippet_search` scoped with `paper_ids=ARXIV:<id>`, two or three per added paper (method, headline
    result, and a third where a number was still unsupported), for all 15 rows added.

- **Errored queries:** none.

- **Date restriction:** none on references. Forward citations were restricted by window as listed above. **Every one of
  the four windows returned exactly 100 results, so each was truncated at the limit, and none was paginated.** The
  `2025-01-01:2025-06-30` window in practice returned only June 2025 papers, so January–May 2025 and all of
  July–December 2025 were **not screened at all**. There is no separate recency sub-pass beyond the `2026-06-01:`
  window, which covers the period since the last pass.

- **Citation traversal:** 2408.03314: 51 references screened of 51, 400 forward citations screened of at least 400 (four
  truncated windows of 100; the true total was not retrieved and is certainly much larger).

- **Scope rule used for screening:** in scope means directly about LLM test-time compute or reasoning methods, verifiers
  and reward models for reasoning, the agent harness, reasoning-model training covered by blocks C–J, or reasoning-model
  safety. Out of scope: test-time scaling applied to other modalities (diffusion, video, vision-language, speech,
  protein), domain applications where test-time compute is not the object of study, inference-systems work, and general
  tech reports. **Screening was by title only**, so near-misses in either direction are possible; titles such as
  "Thinking Costs Tokens" or "Format-Adapter" were judgement calls.

- **New papers added:** 15, all `snippet`-sourced, all with the four dimensions derived from retrieved body text. Block
  A: 2305.10601, 2310.01798, 2303.17651, 2407.18219, 2104.03113, 2411.19477, 2506.04210, 2506.05333. Block B:
  2110.14168, 2211.14275, 2312.08935, 2407.00215, 2411.17501, 2606.00437. Block K: 2303.11366. The 15-row cap was
  binding; selection favoured foundational references first, then forward citations that bear on limits of test-time
  scaling or on verifier robustness.

- **Saturation:** of 199 in-scope candidates screened, 9 were already present in the table (9/199 = 4.5%). By direction:
  references 4/28 = 14% (present: 2201.11903, 2203.14465, 2305.20050, 2402.03300); forward citations 5/171 = 2.9%
  (present: 2609.01274, 2608.20290, 2412.06769, 2410.08146, 2605.14186). Per forward window: `2026-06-01:` 2/39,
  2024-08–12 2/34, June 2025 0/45, 2026-01–05 1/53. Measured against the 150-row table as it stood at the start of this
  traversal; no candidate was added concurrently by another agent. One reference, Villalobos & Atkinson 2023 "Trading
  off compute in training and inference" (an Epoch AI report), is in scope but has no paper ID and is excluded from the
  count.

- **Coverage finding:** the survey is **not** a subset of this neighbourhood. Backward, it was missing the verifier and
  process-supervision foundations the test-time-compute block rests on (Cobbe et al.'s GSM8K verifiers, Uesato et al.'s
  process-vs-outcome comparison, Math-Shepherd), the search and self-correction baselines (Tree of Thoughts,
  Self-Refine, Reflexion, "cannot self-correct yet", RISE), and the pre-LLM train/test compute trade-off (Jones 2021).
  Forward, the citing literature is producing in-scope work far faster than the survey has absorbed it.

- **Corrections to existing rows:** none.

- **Dimension judgement calls:** [2606.00437](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.00437) is tagged `pressure: prompt-level`
  because perturbations are applied to fixed input chains with no training anywhere. It is the closest fit, but it is
  not an in-context attack in the usual sense, and `measures` is `capability` for verifier robustness in the absence of
  a better value. [2110.14168](https://arxiv.org/abs/2110.14168) reports best-of-N degradation beyond 400 samples, which
  the authors attribute to search exploiting the verifier. That is inference-time optimisation pressure on a verifier,
  which none of the four `pressure` values covers, so it is tagged `n/a`. Method papers that train a capability in
  (RISE, Math-Shepherd PPO) are tagged `elicitation: n/a`, following the corpus convention that `elicited` is reserved
  for behaviours of safety interest. [2104.03113](https://arxiv.org/abs/2104.03113) and
  [2506.05333](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.05333) are tagged `fitted` because a fitted scaling relation is the
  contribution; Kinetics' sparse-attention gains are empirical.

- **Stopped because:** the 15-row cap was reached. Neither stopping condition is met. Traversal is incomplete, because
  four forward windows were truncated at 100 and the whole of January–May and July–December 2025 went unscreened.
  Saturation is 4.5% against an 80% threshold. The pass stops on budget, not on coverage.

#### Seed: 2501.12948 (DeepSeek-R1)

- **Preflight:** snippet_search ok | search_papers_by_relevance ok | get_paper ok

- **Seeds:** given by the main session — DeepSeek-R1 (2501.12948), both directions.

- **Two records for one seed.** Semantic Scholar holds DeepSeek-R1 twice, and the citations are split between them. The
  Nature version (`2eed1fad…`, resolved by `ARXIV:2501.12948`) has 28 references and 5,681 citations. The arXiv version
  (`bf76cb3b…`, CorpusId 284488789, found by title search) has 5,460 citations, and its reference list is elided by the
  publisher. Traversing only the record that the arXiv ID resolves to would have missed about half the forward set. Both
  records were traversed. Their union is 10,873 distinct citing papers; 268 titles appear in both.

- **The citation graph is incomplete, so this traversal cannot give a full coverage guarantee.** Only 46 of the 150 rows
  present before this pass appear as citers of either R1 record. Rows that evaluate or build directly on R1, and so
  almost certainly cite it, are absent from both lists: for example 2505.05410, 2504.13837 and 2503.14476. Whether they
  actually cite R1 was not checked against their reference lists. The forward set as indexed is therefore a partial view
  of the papers that actually cite R1. "Not found in R1's forward citations" is evidence about the index, not about the
  literature.

- **Queries run:**

  - `get_paper(ARXIV:2501.12948, references)`: the Nature record's 28 references.
  - `get_citations(ARXIV:2501.12948)`: a probe call. The full forward sets of both records were then pulled from the
    Semantic Scholar Graph API (`/paper/{id}/citations`, pages of 1,000), because Asta's `get_citations` returns at most
    one page per call. This is discovery only; no row is characterised from it.
  - WebFetch of `arxiv.org/html/2501.12948v1` to list the arXiv version's reference list, since Semantic Scholar elides
    it. Discovery only.
  - Title screening of the forward union with three keyword filters, then reading every matched title:
    - Safety and theory filter (faithful, monitor, stegan, obfusc, legib, sandbag, schem, situational, aware, decepti,
      sabotage, control, untrusted, jailbreak, overthink, backdoor, misalign, reward hack, forget, continual,
      composition, expressiv, theor, provabl, dynamics, latent, recurrent, looped, implicit, collapse, self-train,
      self-improv, hidden, covert, honest, verbali, oversight, chain-of-thought, CoT, reasoning trace, safety, attack,
      adversarial, and similar).
    - Capability filter for hand-offs (test-time scal, inference compute/scal, pass@, RLVR, verifiable reward, entropy
      collapse, exploration, process reward, PRM, verifier, scaling law, reasoning boundar, zero RL, harness, scaffold).
    - An exclusion filter for multimodal, robotics, medical and other domain-application titles.
  - 1,627 distinct matched title lines were read. The other ~9,200 titles in the union were not read.
  - Recency sub-pass (`search_papers_by_relevance`, `publication_date_range=2026-03:`):
    - "chain-of-thought monitorability reasoning models obfuscation"
    - "DeepSeek-R1 reasoning trace faithfulness unfaithful chain-of-thought"
    - "theory of chain-of-thought transformers reinforcement learning provable reasoning" (0 results, not an error)
    - "chain-of-thought expressivity transformers theory"
  - Characterisation: two to three `snippet_search` calls per added row, scoped by `paper_ids=ARXIV:<id>`, and one
    `get_paper_batch` call for venue metadata.

- **Errored queries:** two calls, `get_paper(bf76cb3b…)` and `get_paper(CorpusId:284488789)` with `references` fields,
  both returned "'NoneType' object is not iterable". The cause is the publisher-elided reference list (confirmed from
  the raw API response), not rate limiting. Several Semantic Scholar Graph API calls returned 429 and succeeded on retry
  with backoff. None is outstanding.

- **Date restriction:** none on the citation traversal, which covers every date the index holds, 2023 to 2026-11. (The
  late bound postdates this pass, 2026-09-23. It is a Semantic Scholar `publicationDate`, most likely a forward-dated
  journal issue; that reading is an inference, not checked.) The recency sub-pass was restricted to papers from 2026-03
  onward. That window is wider than "since the last pass", because the last pass was the previous day, and a one-day
  window would have tested nothing.

- **Citation traversal:** 2501.12948: 28 references screened of 28 (Nature record, Asta). The arXiv version's roughly 40
  references were also screened from arXiv HTML. 10,873 forward citations were screened by keyword filter, of 10,873
  (5,681 + 5,460 across the two records, minus 268 duplicates). Only the 1,627 filter-matched titles were read.

- **New papers added:** 15, all in owned blocks: F 2507.05386; G 2607.07646; H 2502.02393; I 2502.17416; J 2606.21090; L
  2506.19143, 2602.01017, 2603.05488; M 2505.23575, 2511.11584, 2510.23966; N 2510.09714; P 2502.12893, 2502.02542; Q
  2508.00943. All 15 are `source: snippet`. None is `abstract`.

- **Saturation.** Measured against the survey as it stood before this pass. "In scope" means judged in scope from the
  title after the filters above.

  - Owned blocks (F–J, L–Q), forward citations: of 175 in-scope candidates screened, 26 were already present (26/175 =
    14.9%).
  - Hand-off blocks (A–E, K), forward citations: of 255 candidates, 20 were already present (20/255 = 7.8%). Here "in
    scope" means passing the capability and exclusion filters, not a title-by-title judgement. It is the looser figure
    of the two, and the curated hand-off list in the frontier is a subset.
  - References: of 10 in-scope references, 5 were already present (5/10 = 50%). The five absent were 2211.14275,
    2312.08935, 2210.10760, 2409.12917 and 2309.17179, all belonging in block B or C. Two of them, 2211.14275 and
    2312.08935, were added to block B by the concurrent agent during this pass.
  - Combined: 51/440 = 11.6%.
  - Recency sub-pass, excluded from the stopping rule: of 28 in-scope candidates, 10 were already present (10/28 =
    35.7%).
  - **Reading.** The existing survey is not a subset of R1's neighbourhood. It holds about one in seven of the in-scope
    work in the owned blocks and about one in thirteen of the capability work. Saturation is highest where the survey
    was deliberately built (faithfulness, monitorability, evaluation awareness) and lowest in P (attacks on and defences
    from reasoning) and I (latent reasoning), where 28 and 23 in-scope candidates respectively were found and deferred.
    The recency figure is higher than the traversal figure, which is consistent with the survey leaning towards recent
    safety work.

- **Corrections to existing rows:** none.

- **Dimension judgement calls:**

  - [2511.11584](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.11584) is tagged `pressure: training-incidental`, matching the corpus's
    treatment of [2601.23086](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.23086). The penalty is aimed at an output monitor, not at the
    CoT monitor being evaluated. It is much closer to that monitor than a length penalty would be, though, and
    `monitor-in-reward` is arguable.
  - [2602.01017](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.01017) is tagged `elicitation: spontaneous`. Unfaithfulness arises from
    ordinary next-token training that never targets it, but the setting is a toy the authors built, with training noise
    as their dial.
  - [2506.19143](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.19143) and [2603.05488](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.05488) are tagged
    `measures: faithfulness`. Neither uses cue injection. The first tests whether trace sentences causally drive the
    answer; the second tests whether the trace discloses beliefs that probes can read.
  - [2510.23966](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.23966) is tagged `elicitation: observational`, `pressure: none`. It scores
    the default traces of existing models, and its authors state it is not an adversarial test.
  - [2510.09714](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.09714) is tagged `measures: capability`: it measures the capability that
    encoded reasoning would need, not monitorability itself.
  - [2502.17416](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.17416) is tagged `evidence: empirical`, although its CoT-simulation theorem
    is proven.

- **Process note:** one edit to this file was made with a scripted in-place string replacement rather than `Edit`. It
  changed the venue cells of 2505.23575, 2508.00943 and 2502.02393 to the Semantic Scholar venue metadata. Row count and
  duplicate checks passed immediately afterwards.

- **Stopped because:** the 15-row cap was reached. Neither stopping condition is met. The references were traversed
  completely, but the forward traversal read only the keyword-matched titles, and the index itself is missing citers.
  Measured saturation is 14.9% (owned blocks) and 7.8% (hand-off blocks), far below the 80% threshold. The pass stops on
  budget, not on coverage.

## Frontier

Outstanding work, kept here rather than in a session's context so that it survives a session ending mid-pass. Current as
of pass 2026-09-23.

**Seeds not yet traversed**

The seeds actually used were chosen by judgement. The repo's stated criterion — highest in-degree within the retrieved
candidate set — was applied retroactively afterwards, computing, for each of the 150 papers, how many of the other 149
cite it. All 150 returned reference lists. Because roughly 45% of the corpus carries 2026 arXiv IDs whose reference
graphs Semantic Scholar has not fully resolved, these counts are **lower bounds**, and the newest third is undercounted
relative to the oldest.

The procedure does **not** reproduce the seeds used. Ranked by within-set in-degree, the traversed seeds place 5th (Yue,
29), 6th-equal (Korbak, 28) and 8th (Snell, 26; traversed in pass 2026-09-23). Four papers outrank all three, and a
literal application of the criterion would have selected mostly older foundational work. This is recorded rather than
rationalised: it means the stopping rule, which is defined over the seeds, rests on a seed set the criterion would not
have produced.

Traversal status of the in-degree-nominated seeds, in priority order. Pass 2026-09-23 traversed the first three; none is
exhausted:

- [DeepSeekMath / GRPO](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.03300) — in-degree 48, rank 2. References complete (57/57). Forward
  citations **sampled**, not exhausted: 754 screened of 8,950. Six date-sliced calls fetched 4,677, and four of those
  slices hit the 1,000-per-call cap in index order, so the unfetched remainder is not known to be low-impact.
- [Snell et al., *Scaling LLM Test-Time Compute Optimally*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.03314) — in-degree 26, rank 8.
  References complete (51/51, recovered from arXiv HTML where Semantic Scholar resolved only 32). Forward citations
  **sampled**: 400 screened, four date windows each capped at 100 without pagination, and the 2025-01–05 and 2025-07–12
  windows not screened at all. Those two windows are the largest single hole in the pass.
- [DeepSeek-R1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.12948) — in-degree 25, rank 9-equal. Semantic Scholar splits it across two
  records; both were traversed. References complete for the Nature record (28/28), plus about 40 from the arXiv
  version's reference list. All 10,873 forward citers were keyword-filtered and 1,627 titles read; the other roughly
  9,200 were never read. The index is missing many citers it should have, so this traversal is a lower bound.
- [Korbak et al.](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.11473) — forward citations were capped at the 100 returned by a single
  query and not paginated. Backward references are complete.
- [Turpin et al.](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.04388) — in-degree 28, tied 6th, the highest-ranked safety-cluster paper
  alongside Korbak. A strict reading of the criterion would have made it a seed.

Lower priority despite topping the ranking: [CoT prompting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2201.11903) (58) and
[Self-Consistency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2203.11171) (34) are canonical 2022 papers whose own references mostly predate
this survey and whose forward-citation sets are enormous and largely out of scope. High in-degree here reflects their
being universally cited, not their being hubs of this topic.

**Cluster structure.** The in-degree ranking surfaces a third cluster the input did not name: an RL-training-recipe
group ([GRPO](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.03300), [DAPO](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.14476),
[DeepSeek-R1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.12948), [Dr. GRPO](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.20783)) distinct from
inference-time scaling and from safety. No seed was chosen to cover it.

**Screened but not added** — all judged in scope; deferred when the 15-paper per-sub-pass budget was reached.

setup and direction of effect were confirmed in body text. In each, the headline number could not be confirmed, and the
cell says so explicitly. Read literally, the "never upgrade on a partial check" rule in `CLAUDE.md` would put these rows
at `abstract`. That would misreport the method cells, which were checked. The rule does not yet distinguish these cases,
so they are listed here rather than resolved silently.

- A number taken from the abstract inside an otherwise snippet-checked cell, flagged inline: 2603.05488 (early-exit
  token savings).

The remaining 17 `abstract`-sourced rows are not cited in the output and need nothing until they are.

All of these are 2026 preprints with no open-access full text indexed by Asta, so this is a limitation of the index
rather than an outstanding retrieval task. Re-check periodically; indexing lags publication.
